{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1","id":"msg_011CgM4uPbWz9oivGmGwpzTV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Cahn-Hilliard benchmark now runs entirely on the GPU with CUDA. Only the two existing files changed, the executable is still `cahn_hilliard`, and no new dependencies were added.\n\n**What changed in `cahn_hilliard.cpp`**\n\n- Both passes of each time step, the chemical potential and the concentration update, run as instances of one templated 7-point Laplacian stencil kernel with a per-cell functor. Clamped boundaries are preserved by index clamping in the halo loads.\n- Each thread block owns a 32x8 XY tile and sweeps a chunk of Z slices. Planes are staged in shared memory using three rotating buffers so each slice needs a single barrier, with the z-1 value carried in a register.\n- The Z chunk size is chosen at runtime from the SM count so that small grids still produce enough blocks to fill the GPU.\n- Field initialization runs on the device with the same integer arithmetic as before. The three field buffers live on the GPU for the whole run, and only the final field is copied back for results printing and validation.\n- The bulk term of the chemical potential is folded into two host-precomputed coefficients, which cuts FP64 instructions on this FP64-limited consumer GPU. Divisions by dx², dy², dz² became multiplications by precomputed reciprocals.\n- Kernel modules are preloaded before the timer starts. Timing wraps the step loop plus a device synchronize, matching the original measurement.\n\n**What changed in `CMakeLists.txt`**\n\nThe project enables the CUDA language, compiles the existing .cpp file as CUDA via a source property, defaults to a Release build, and targets SM 70 through 90 unless the architecture is set externally.\n\n**Verification against the original CPU build**\n\nSum, min, max, and the sample values agree to roughly 1e-16 on the default 64³ case, on 128³ with 50 steps, and on odd non-cubic grids such as 100x50x30, 17x9x5, and 1x1x1. The byte-level hash differs, which is unavoidable since the CPU build already uses FMA contraction and the GPU rounds differently at the last bit. Validation passes on all tested sizes, including 512³.\n\n| Grid, steps | CPU original | GPU (RTX 3090) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 128³, 50 | 153 MCU/s | ~8,700 MCU/s |\n| 512³, 50 | not run | ~9,700 MCU/s |\n\nProfiling showed the chemical-potential kernel is bound by FP64 throughput on this card rather than bandwidth, so further gains would need a datacenter-class GPU or reduced precision, which would change results."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":228,"cache_read_input_tokens":44707,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":228},"output_tokens":704,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c460c5f3-d0ce-44b9-9797-2ebe3e6e88fb","uuid":"f808ac00-da8d-494f-893f-ff18687bb1a0","timestamp":"2026-10-24T12:33:11.473Z","request_id":"req_011CgM4uPK9q3taC2pV9yHnD"}
